### 3. ARC2020 numbers, item by item

- 416×416: paper line 111 "YOLOv3-tiny accepts an RGB image of 416 × 416 resolution as input." ✅
- 16-bit: line 549-552 "8-bit and 16-bit wordlength... The results led to the adoption of 16-bits wordlength"; line 642 "16b" in table; line 699 "16bit fixed point implementation without any retraining". ✅
- 532 ms: line 591 "latency of 532ms per inference (measured on the board)". ✅
- 1.88 fps: line 697 "achieves a frame rate of 1.88 fps". ✅ (note: 1/0.532 = 1.8797 → consistent)
- 10.45 GOPS: line 675 table GOPS 10.45; line 697 "throughput of 10.45 GOPS". ✅
- 160 DSP: line 591 "160 DSPs"; table 657. ✅
- 185 BRAM18: line 591 "185 BRAMs"; table header line 646 "BRAM18k" 185. ✅ (paper says "185 BRAMs"; table col "BRAM18k" → 185 BRAM18. Doc's "185 BRAM18" acceptable.)
- 25.9k LUT: line 591 "25.9k LUTs". ✅
- 46.7k FF: line 591 "46.7k FFs". ✅
- 3.36 W: line 592 "measured power consumption is 3.36W". ✅
- mAP 30.9% vs 33.4%: line 553-554 & 698. ✅
- Clock PL 100 MHz / PS 666.7 MHz: line 585-586 "The clock frequency of programmable logic and processing system is 100 MHz and 666.7MHz respectively." ✅
- Zedboard + 512 MB DDR3: line 584 "A Zedboard development kit with Xilinx XC7Z020 SoC and 512 MB DDR3". ✅

But: doc line 69 says "上一代工作在 Zynq-7035（900 DSP，比 7020 大 4 倍）上也只有 52 ms". Paper: Wei et al 52 ms on Zynq 7035, table DSP 409 (line 653: [18] DSP 409). ### 5. §3.1 extrapolation table

Doc line 73: "YOLOv3-tiny @416 ≈ 2.8 G MAC ≈ 5.6 GOP/帧。按实测 10.45 GOPS 线性外推（**估算，须标注为估算**）"

Check arithmetic: 2.8 G MAC = 5.6 GOP (2 ops per MAC) ✓ consistent. Paper: 10.45 GOPS, 532 ms → ops per frame = 10.45e9 × 0.532 = 5.559e9 ≈ 5.56 GOP ✓. So the 5.6 GOP is derivable from the paper (10.45 GOPS × 532ms). Good — actually that's a nice check: 5.6 GOP is *consistent with* paper. And 2.8 GMAC = 5.6 GOP.

Now the table rows:
- 224×224: doc says ≈1.63 GOP. Scaling by area: (224/416)² = 0.2899; 5.6 × 0.2899 = 1.623 ✓. Latency 156 ms: 1.63/5.6 × 532 = 155.4 ✓ (i.e., 0.29×532=154.3; they used 156 = 1.63/10.45e9? 1.63e9/10.45e9 = 156 ms ✓). fps 6.4: 1000/156 = 6.41 ✓.
- 160×160: (160/416)² = 0.1479; 5.6×0.1479 = 0.828 ✓ 0.83. 0.83/10.45 = 79.4 ms ✓ 79. fps 12.7 = 1000/79 ✓.
- YOLO-Fastest @320: "≈0.2–0.3 GMAC" → GOP 0.5 (they took 0.25 GMAC = 0.5 GOP). 0.5/10.45 = 47.8 ms ≈48 ✓. fps 20.8 → "20+" ✓.

But: is it purely linear extrapolation? Two nonlinearities: (a) fixed overheads (per-layer DMA, control, Tcpu) don't scale with area — so latency scaling is sub-linear, meaning estimates are optimistic; (b) the 10.45 GOPS throughput was measured at a DSE point optimized for 416; at smaller resolutions the design point/parallelism would differ. Also (c) resolution scaling of YOLOv3-tiny isn't exactly quadratic: the fully-connected-free conv net is roughly quadratic but downsampling schedule/strides at different input sizes changes feature map sizes non-uniformly (e.g., 416/32=13, 224/32=7, 160/32=5 — integer division effects; 224 → 7×7 & 14×14 vs expected 7.0/14.0 fine; 160/32=5 exactly). Also the doc claims "线性外推" but actually does *area* scaling (quadratic), which is not linear. That's a terminology issue: they call it 线性外推 but compute quadratic scaling. Worth flagging as 🔶/⚠️ terminology but really: the extrapolation is "compute-linear" (latency ∝ GOP at constant GOPS) — that IS linear in GOP, so "线性外推" is defensible. I'll note it's linear in *operations*, not in resolution, and that it ignores fixed per-batch overhead → optimistic bias. The doc's own caveat at line 164 says "除 416 行外均为线性外推估算" — that's a proper label. So verdict for the table: mostly ⚠️估算 but properly labeled... The task asks: "§3.1 的外推表（224/160/320 分辨率）是不是纯线性外推" — answer: yes, linear in GOP at constant 10.45 GOPS throughput; arithmetic internally consistent; but (a) it inherits the 5.6 GOP which is itself consistent with paper; (b) ignores fixed overhead → optimistic; (c) the YOLO-Fastest 0.2–0.3 GMAC input is un-sourced. Also worth noting the doc says "估算，须标注为估算" — that's odd phrasing (a note to self) — it IS labeled in §164.

Hmm, one more: the table's 224 row says "≈1.63" — area scaling assumes conv ops ∝ H×W exactly. True for conv layers. OK.

Hmm, but is "10.45 GOPS" the *throughput* = ops/latency? Paper calls it "throughput of 10.45 GOPS" in conclusion. 5.56 GOP/0.532s = 10.45 GOP/s exactly. So the paper's GOPS is derived from same numbers, not measured independently. So the doc's use of it as "实测持续算力" then re-multiplied to get 5.6 GOP is circular! Important finding: doc line 33 says "实测持续算力 ≈ 10.45 GOP/s" and line 73 uses 10.45 GOPS to extrapolate; but 10.45 = 5.56GOP/0.532s, i.e., it is computed from the same 532 ms. So the extrapolation isn't adding independent information — it's just re-scaling 532 ms by GOP ratio. That's a legitimate methodological note: the "anchor" is one data point; 10.45 GOPS is not an independent throughput measurement (it's consistent only if 5.56 GOP is the true op count, which the paper doesn't state — the paper never gives the operation count!).

Also §3.1's linear extrapolation assumption: 10.45 GOPS held constant while ARC uses 160 DSP; but at lower resolution the DSP usage would drop (less parallelism), so constant-GOPS is optimistic; also PS-side overhead is fixed, so latency won't scale linearly. Worth noting as caveat (估算未含固定开销 → 偏乐观). Also 2.8 G MAC → the extrapolation table's 5.6 GOP at 416 gives 532ms exactly by construction (5.6/10.45=536ms) — i.e., the 416 row wasn't taken from the paper but recomputed, coincidentally consistent (536 vs 532).

Key numeric claims to audit:
1. 5.6 MB (L27, L113) — unit confusion with 5.6 GOP/帧. Real: 8.85 M params → INT8 8.85 MB. My recount gives 5.565 GOP/frame which reproduces the paper's 10.45 GOPS × 0.532 s = 5.559 GOP exactly. That's a strong result: the paper's throughput×latency implies 5.56 GOP/frame, and my independent cfg-based recount of 2.7825 GMAC = 5.565 GOP matches to 0.1%. So the doc's "2.8 G MAC ≈ 5.6 GOP/帧" is CORRECT ✅ (2.78 GMAC → 5.565 GOP). 

Wait — earlier round said 6.77 GMAC / 13.5 GOP. That was the buggy version (conv6/conv7 at 26×26 and conv11 1024→256). The cleaned recount (conv6 at 13, conv11 256→128) gives 2.7825 GMAC = 5.565 GOP, which matches the paper exactly. So the earlier "低估 2.42×" conclusion was WRONG and must be discarded. Good thing I redid it.

Let me double check conv6 resolution: input 416 → conv1 (416) → pool 208 → conv2 208 → pool 104 → conv3 104 → pool 52 → conv4 52 → pool 26 → conv5 26 → pool 13 → conv6 @13. Yes conv6 is @13, not 26. Correct: 5 maxpools before conv6. And conv7 @13 (maxpool stride 1). Correct.

conv11: route -4 from conv9... let's trace: after conv9 (13×13, 256ch? no conv9 is 256→512). Layers list index: [conv1, mp, conv2, mp, conv3, mp, conv4, mp, conv5, mp, conv6, mp(stride1), conv7, conv8, conv9, conv10, mp(stride1), route -4, conv11, upsample, route -1,8, conv12, conv13].

Actually darknet yolov3-tiny cfg:
```
0 conv 3->16
1 maxpool
2 conv 16->32
3 maxpool
4 conv 32->64
5 maxpool
6 conv 64->128
7 maxpool
8 conv 128->256
9 maxpool
10 conv 256->512
11 maxpool size=2 stride=1
12 conv 512->1024
13 conv 1024->256 (1x1)
14 conv 256->512
15 conv 512->255 (1x1) -> yolo
16 maxpool stride 1
17 route -4  -> layer 13
18 conv 128 1x1
19 upsample
20 route -1, 8 -> upsample + layer 8 (256ch)
21 conv 384->256 3x3
22 conv 256->255 1x1 -> yolo
```
Yes: layer 18 conv 1024? no — input to layer 18 is layer 13 output = 256 ch, so conv 256→128 k1. Correct as in my clean script. And route -1,8: upsample of 128ch + layer 8 (256ch) = 384. Correct.

So params 8.85 M, MAC 2.78 GMAC. Great — this matches the paper's implied 5.56 GOP. Excellent.

So the doc's "≈2.8 G MAC ≈ 5.6 GOP/帧" ✅ correct. And the paper doesn't state it but it's derivable — but is it "一手可核"? The cfg isn't local evidence; I got it from web. Hmm. Now the 5.6 MB: real INT8 = 8.85 MB. So 5.6 MB is not just unit confusion — it's also numerically wrong by 1.58×. Both. Good.

Note: 5.6 MB could also coincide with... hmm 5.6 M params? No. It's clearly copied from 5.6 GOP. Judgment on "单位串用": likely yes, given identical numeral, and the doc's own §3.1 gives 5.6 GOP. But magnitude: INT8 8.85 MB, FP16 17.7 MB, FP32 35.4 MB. So "5.6 MB" understates real INT8 by 1.58×.

5. §3.1 extrapolation: 224 and 160 rows are pure area-linear (1.614 vs claimed 1.63; 0.823 vs 0.83) — tiny rounding. So yes, pure linear scaling of GOP with pixel count, and latency/fps derived at 10.45 GOPS constant throughput. The doc does label it "估算，须标注为估算" at L73 and 未验证项 ① at L164. So partially labeled. BUT: 320 row is NOT linear from 416 — it's a different model (YOLO-Fastest), 0.5 GOP. Labeled "公开数据 ≈0.2–0.3 GMAC，需自行复测" — 0.2–0.3 GMAC ×2 = 0.4–0.6 GOP, so 0.5 is within range ✅. But then 48 ms at 10.45 GOPS = 0.5/10.45 = 47.8 ms ✅ consistent. And 20 fps = 1/0.048 = 20.8 ✅. So the 320 row is internally consistent given the 0.5 GOP assumption; it's just not a resolution-scaling of the same model. Doc's 未验证项 ① says "除 416 行外均为线性外推估算" — that's imprecise/wrong for the 320 row (it's a different model, not a linear extrapolation). Minor 🔶? It's a mislabel: the table implies all rows scale the same model; 320 row swaps model. I'd flag as 🔶 internal inconsistency (small): 未验证项① says all non-416 rows are linear extrapolations, but the 320 row can't be one (linear would be 3.3 GOP / 315 ms / 3.2 fps).

Also, a real methodological point: linear latency extrapolation assumes constant 10.45 GOPS regardless of resolution — but at lower resolution, utilization typically drops (fixed overheads dominate), so 6.4/12.7 fps are optimistic. Not a contradiction though; it's an estimate. I'll note it as a caveat.
